=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a string initialCurrency, and you start with 1.0 of initialCurrency.
You are also given four arrays with currency pairs (strings) and rates (real numbers):

pairs1[i] = [startCurrency_i, targetCurrency_i] denotes that you can convert from startCurrency_i to targetCurrency_i at a rate of rates1[i] on day 1.
pairs2[i] = [startCurrency_i, targetCurrency_i] denotes that you can convert from startCurrency_i to targetCurrency_i at a rate of rates2[i] on day 2.
Also, each targetCurrency can be converted back to its corresponding startCurrency at a rate of 1 / rate.

You can perform any number of conversions, including zero, using rates1 on day 1, followed by any number of additional conversions, including zero, using rates2 on day 2.
Return the maximum amount of initialCurrency you can have after performing any number of conversions on both days in order.
Note: Conversion rates are valid, and there will be no contradictions in the rates for either day. The rates for the days are independent of each other.
 
Example 1:

Input: initialCurrency = "EUR", pairs1 = [["EUR","USD"],["USD","JPY"]], rates1 = [2.0,3.0], pairs2 = [["JPY","USD"],["USD","CHF"],["CHF","EUR"]], rates2 = [4.0,5.0,6.0]
Output: 720.00000
Explanation:
To get the maximum amount of EUR, starting with 1.0 EUR:

On Day 1:
	
Convert EUR to USD to get 2.0 USD.
Convert USD to JPY to get 6.0 JPY.


On Day 2:
	
Convert JPY to USD to get 24.0 USD.
Convert USD to CHF to get 120.0 CHF.
Finally, convert CHF to EUR to get 720.0 EUR.




Example 2:

Input: initialCurrency = "NGN", pairs1 = [["NGN","EUR"]], rates1 = [9.0], pairs2 = [["NGN","EUR"]], rates2 = [6.0]
Output: 1.50000
Explanation:
Converting NGN to EUR on day 1 and EUR to NGN using the inverse rate on day 2 gives the maximum amount.

Example 3:

Input: initialCurrency = "USD", pairs1 = [["USD","EUR"]], rates1 = [1.0], pairs2 = [["EUR","JPY"]], rates2 = [10.0]
Output: 1.00000
Explanation:
In this example, there is no need to make any conversions on either day.

 
Constraints:

1 <= initialCurrency.length <= 3
initialCurrency consists only of uppercase English letters.
1 <= n == pairs1.length <= 10
1 <= m == pairs2.length <= 10
pairs1[i] == [startCurrency_i, targetCurrency_i]
pairs2[i] == [startCurrency_i, targetCurrency_i]
1 <= startCurrency_i.length, targetCurrency_i.length <= 3
startCurrency_i and targetCurrency_i consist only of uppercase English letters.
rates1.length == n
rates2.length == m
1.0 <= rates1[i], rates2[i] <= 10.0
The input is generated such that there are no contradictions or cycles in the conversion graphs for either day.
The input is generated such that the output is at most 5 * 10^10.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxAmount(self, initialCurrency: str, pairs1: List[List[str]], rates1: List[float], pairs2: List[List[str]], rates2: List[float]) -> float:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Pseudo:

from typing import List
class Solution:
    def maxAmount(self, initialCurrency: str, pairs1: List[List[str]], rates1: List[float], pairs2: List[List[str]], rates2: List[float]) -> float:
        def reachable_factors(start, pairs, rates):
            adj = defaultdict(list)
            for (a,b), r in zip(pairs, rates):
                adj[a].append((b, r))
                adj[b].append((a, 1.0 / r))
            factor = {start: 1.0}
            stack = [start]
            while stack:
                u = stack.pop()
                fu = factor[u]
                for v, w in adj.get(u, []):
                    if v not in factor:
                        factor[v] = fu * w
                        stack.append(v)
            return factor

        day1 = reachable_factors(initialCurrency, pairs1, rates1)
        day2 = reachable_factors(initialCurrency, pairs2, rates2)
        ans = 1.0
        for cur, f1 in day1.items():
            if cur in day2:
                ans = max(ans, f1 / day2[cur])  # because day2[cur] = amount initialCurrency per 1 initial? Wait day2 factor from initial to cur. To convert 1 cur to initial, multiply by 1/day2[cur]. Yes.
        return ans

Need check day2 factor: reachable_factors(start=initial) returns factor for each cur = amount of cur per 1 initial using day2. If we have amount cur = f1 (per 1 initial after day1), converting cur -> initial on day2: amount initial = f1 * (1 / factor_initial_to_cur). So f1 / day2[cur]. Good.

Now, final code:

```python
from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def maxAmount(self, initialCurrency: str, pairs1: List[List[str]], rates1: List[float], pairs2: List[List[str]], rates2: List[float]) -> float:
        def get_factors(start: str, pairs: List[List[str]], rates: List[float]) -> dict:
            adj = defaultdict(list)
            for (a, b), r in zip(pairs, rates):
                adj[a].append((b, r))
                adj[b].append((a, 1.0 / r))
            
            factor = {start: 1.0}
            stack = [start]
            while stack:
                u = stack.pop()
                fu = factor[u]
                for v, w in adj.get(u, []):
                    if v not in factor:
                        factor[v] = fu * w
                        stack.append(v)
            return factor
        
        day1 = get_factors(initialCurrency, pairs1, rates1)
        day2 = get_factors(initialCurrency, pairs2, rates2)
        
        ans = 1.0
        for cur, f1 in day1.items():
            if cur in day2:
                ans = max(ans, f1 / day2[cur])
        return ans
```

```
def maxAmount(initialCurrency, pairs1, rates1, pairs2, rates2):
    currencies = set([initialCurrency])
    for a,b in pairs1+pairs2:
        currencies.add(a); currencies.add(b)
    idx = {c:i for i,c in enumerate(currencies)}
    n = len(currencies)
    dist1 = [[0]*n for _ in range(n)]
    dist2 = [[0]*n for _ in range(n)]
    for i in range(n):
        dist1[i][i]=dist2[i][i]=1
    for (a,b),r in zip(pairs1, rates1):
        i,j=idx[a],idx[b]
        dist1[i][j]=max(dist1[i][j], r)
        dist1[j][i]=max(dist1[j][i], 1/r)
    ...
    for k in range(n):
      for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
          if dist[i][k] and dist[k][j]:
             dist[i][j]=max(dist[i][j], dist[i][k]*dist[k][j])
    ans=1
    for c in currencies:
       i=idx[c]
       ans=max(ans, dist1[idx[initial]][i] * dist2[i][idx[initial]])
    return ans
```
